A grown-up-sized long-sleeved, ankle-length, button-front union-suit style onesie, hoodless, with a ribbed collar, with attached gloves sewn into the sleeves, and with sewn-in bootie feet, in thermal knit, a real plain stretch suit and not a real garment, covered edge to edge in a comic-style inked illustration print, designed to make the onesie look like the outfit of a grapple-line scout in a masculine-styled version. The outfit's design is wrapped around the entire suit as one continuous print from the collar to the ankles, wrapping around the sides to the back. The print shows a brown cloak over a harness with grappling gear, trousers, boots printed on the feet, and printed gloves on the attached gloves at the sleeve ends. The sleeve hems are cut at an angle, slanted, pointed or curved, to follow the outfit's own cuffs, and the print follows that edge exactly. Turn it round and the back carries the rear of the outfit, with nothing left plain. There is no separate background, the print is cut to the onesie's shape. The print is applied to the suit's own pattern pieces, front, back, each sleeve and each leg are fully covered in the matching cloth of the outfit, with no leftover background colour between them. There are no printed arms, hands, fingers, legs or feet and no printed skin on the suit, except a small printed neckline detail where the outfit is open. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's cloth runs unbroken from the collar band across both shoulders and down both sleeves to their hems, so every part of the suit is outfit cloth. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. The hood carries only hood cloth, no face, hair, ears or head is printed on it, and no crowd, audience, stage, lights or scene appears anywhere on the suit, only the outfit's own cloth. It may also show a printed glimpse of bare midriff in __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ with a printed navel and light belly hair, all as flat ink. A printed waistband showing only the top of trousers. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the hood trim in faux leather, the patches and patches in quilted padding and the belt and buckle in felt, standing out from the surface. The print gives the whole outfit, and raised layers of soft fabric are sewn on over it for the collar, pockets, belt, buttons and patches, so the suit is half picture and half sculpture. It is a complete costume of grapple-line scout in a masculine-styled version in one piece, shown full length from collar to ankles, with a brown cloak over a harness with grappling gear, trousers on the legs, boots (as the feet), twin blades sewn or attached to the suit. The upper half carries the top and the leg tubes carry the lower garment, so the whole outfit reads at once. Novelty embellishments, knitted rib panels with raised cable texture, scalloped felt edging, beaded fringe along the hem and oversized novelty buttons in contrasting colours. Everything is chosen to be fun to look at and tactile to feel, bumpy, fluffy, shiny and soft in turn.

The print shows a fitted tunic with a gold sash, trousers, with no footwear shown because the suit is footless, and the sleeves end in plain cuffs with no hands printed. Turn it round and the back carries the rear of the outfit, with nothing left plain. No background colour or plain jersey shows around the print, on the shoulders, sleeves or legs. The outfit's cloth wraps the whole suit like a pattern, with the body, the outside and inside of each sleeve and each leg, and the sides, so there are no plain coloured side panels or background patches anywhere. Where the outfit leaves arms or legs bare, the suit itself is cut sleeveless, short-sleeved or short-legged at that point, so the wearer's own limbs show; no printed arm, leg, hand, foot or skin appears on the suit's fabric. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The print is cut exactly to the suit, neck, shoulders, armholes, sleeves and hem are all covered in the outfit's own fabric. The outline is exactly a onesie's, nothing sticks out past the suit's edges, and no coat tails, skirts, capes, extra sleeves or loose layers hang outside it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. No standing figure, torso, arms or legs are depicted; the suit's own shape is the body, and the print is only the outfit's fabric covering it from edge to edge. It may also show flat printed skin in __clothing_uniform_tee_skin__ at the neckline, with fine printed body hair where it would naturally show, all as flat ink. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the straps and holsters in fleece, the medals and ribbons in faux fur and the patches and patches in felt, standing out from the surface. The print gives the whole outfit, and raised layers of soft fabric are sewn on over it for the collar, pockets, belt, buttons and patches, so the suit is half picture and half sculpture. It is a complete costume of ship's captain in a masculine-styled version in one piece, shown full length from collar to ankles, with a fitted tunic with a gold sash, trousers on the legs, boots (as the feet), a data pad sewn or attached to the suit. The upper half carries the top and the leg tubes carry the lower garment, so the whole outfit reads at once. Novelty embellishments, chunky pom-pom trim along the hood edge and cuffs, oversized novelty buttons in contrasting colours, glitter-flecked tulle layered at the hem and corduroy ridged panels. Everything is chosen to be fun to look at and tactile to feel, bumpy, fluffy, shiny and soft in turn.

Front and back are both fully printed, the back showing how the outfit looks from behind. No background colour or plain jersey shows around the print, on the shoulders, sleeves or legs. The outfit's cloth wraps the whole suit like a pattern, with the body, the outside and inside of each sleeve and each leg, and the sides, so there are no plain coloured side panels or background patches anywhere. Where the outfit leaves arms or legs bare, the suit itself is cut sleeveless, short-sleeved or short-legged at that point, so the wearer's own limbs show; no printed arm, leg, hand, foot or skin appears on the suit's fabric. Every part of the shirt, shoulders and sleeves included, shows the outfit's own colours and cloth texture in one uniform print. The outfit's collar meets the real collar band, its shoulders sit on the real shoulder seams and its sleeves fill the real sleeves, all in one piece of printed cloth. The silhouette is a plain one-piece suit, with every part of the outfit printed inside its shape and none beyond it. The outfit is laid out on the suit's own rectangle shape, fully covering it from side seam to side seam. The printed parts are flat ink on stretch jersey. No standing figure, torso, arms or legs are depicted; the suit's own shape is the body, and the print is only the outfit's fabric covering it from edge to edge. Raised plush layers are sewn on over the print, the belt and buckle in faux fur, the patches and patches in chenille and the buttons in velvet, standing out from the surface. Printed and plush together, the outfit is printed all over, with padded appliqué parts standing out from the surface.
